Valid ame inference under such designs: bootstrap standard errors, IM p-values (*impvalues* — cross-cluster dispersion inference, which is not disabled by the cell-containment screen; note that it carries its own under-coverage, described under *Discussion of Ibragimov and Muller-Style Inference*, and requires a `cluster()` that nests *i*), randomization inference, *robust* (or a robust *customvce()*) when errors are independent across observations (not appropriate under within-unit serial dependence), or clustering on a variable strictly finer than the cohort-by-period cells. Note that binary-treatment (att) standard errors under cohort-level clustering are also somewhat understated on such saturated designs (a milder, documented phenomenon); wooldid does not suppress them. On some of these designs the estimation command returns an ALL-ZERO covariance outright, in which case no standard error in the run is identified: *wooldid* says so and continues, the reported estimates stand, and every standard error, test statistic, p-value and interval is missing.

## 11. Performance and technical options

### The levers, in order of leverage

1. **Coarsen `i`** (see chapter 2). Runtime is driven by the cohort-by-period cell count and grows faster than linearly in it; sample size costs only linearly. This is the largest speedup available and also repairs the inference problems of a fine `i`.
2. **Narrow the event-study window.** Cost is proportional to the horizons requested.
3. **`fastnose`** skips every variance calculation, which is most of a `margins` call, and is worth several times over for specification search. Refused with the options that need standard errors; re-run without it before reporting.
4. **The default cell collapse** evaluates every `margins` call on one row per cohort-by-period-by-treatment cell instead of every observation. It is on whenever the request permits, reproduces the full-data results to *margins*' own accuracy (typically machine precision; see *nofastcollapse*), and is skipped under a caution for the ineligible requests (`interactivecontrols()`, `contreatpoly()` above 1, the dose variable inside a controls option, `unconditionalse`). Turn it off with `nofastcollapse` when you use controls or fixed effects interacted with treatment in a way wooldid cannot detect (a renamed copy of the dose, for example), when memory is tight (the collapse `preserve`s the data), or when you need byte-identical reproduction of an uncollapsed run.
5. **`lattice()`** is the most expensive axis; keep `latticenum` small.
6. **Replications.** A `bootstrap()` or `permute()` run costs the base run times the replication count; subsampling does not help, coarsening `i` and narrowing the window do. Use a small `reps()` (no minimum) or `permreps()` (at least 20) to time a specification, then raise them.
7. **`emptycellsoverride`** roughly doubles runtime and is only for inspecting the raw regression under `verbose`. `regtol()` tightens `reghdfe`'s tolerance (a larger number is tighter; default 9). `set maxvar` raises Stata's variable limit when a granular `i` hits r(907); wooldid pre-checks this under default safety.

`update` refreshes the installation from GitHub (restart Stata or `discard` afterwards); `verbose` prints the regression command and stores the fitted model as `___wooldidfullmodel` (coefficient-level replay only); `cleanmatrices` clears Stata and Mata matrices when `reghdfe` leaves the session in a bad state; `oldsyntax` restores the old `contemp` coefficient suffix and is refused by both replication engines.

The rest of this chapter is the help file's account of `fastnose`, the cell collapse, the runtime drivers and the technical options.

### From the help file

`fast:nose`: Fast exploratory mode. Under *fastnose*: *e(V)* is not posted (*e(properties)* is *b*, and the disclosure marker *e(wdid_vcvstate)* reads *absent*), so *test*, *lincom*, *_se[]*, *estat vce* and anything else needing a variance-covariance matrix will refuse. *esttab* and *estout* do **not** refuse, and under a bare *fastnose* they print a coefficient with an empty inference row beneath their usual footer, so read the table rather than trusting the footer. Under the `permute()` fast-mode default they do better than that: the run posts *e(p)*, *e(ci_l)* and *e(ci_u)* under the names *estout* reads **ahead** of anything derived from *e(V)*, so `esttab, p` and `esttab, ci` carry the run's real permutation p-values and shift intervals, with truthful footers, and a plain `esttab` tables the coefficients. No *e(se)* is posted there and none is invented: a permutation run has no standard error to report; *e(V_es_)* is not returned, so *honestdid* and Roth-style diagnostics have no covariance to work from, while *e(b_es_)* and *e(numpre_)* **are** returned - the estimates are meaningful even where their uncertainty is unavailable, and a Caution per family says so (under the `permute()` fast-mode default none of the three is returned); no joint tests of any kind are computed (including the subgroup homogeneity tests); the one exception is the subgroup-by-horizon margins call, which keeps its variance so that the estimability screen described under *subgroups()* still drops a non-estimable horizon (fast mode therefore reports the same event-study rows as the full run, at a small cost per subgroup); the continuous-treatment-effects plot matrices carry only the point-estimate column, exactly as under *cfxplotnose*; and event-study plots are drawn without confidence bands. `simultaneousbands` is accepted rather than refused, but no band is built - the analytic engine needs the event-study covariance fast mode does not compute - and a caution says so; the pointwise interval columns are unaffected and no *e(esband_*)* results are returned. Not compatible with *unconditionalse*, *impvalues*, *pretrendpower()* or `bootstrap()` (each is refused with an error), nor with *permtstat* or *permtci*, which randomize on t statistics fast mode does not compute. *adversarial* and *jointtests* are likewise refused, **except** under the `permute()` fast-mode default described next (no `permtstat`, no `permanalyticse`), where fast mode is already in force and naming it is accepted rather than an error. Under `permute()` without `permtstat` the observed run takes this fast path by default - the permutation inference does not consume the analytic standard errors - and `permanalyticse` restores them; the adversarial and joint-test surfaces are then permutation quantities built after the replications, not the analytic ones fast mode lacks. Point estimates can differ from a full run in roughly the last digit of precision (absolute differences on the order of 1e-15; relative differences are typically similar but can reach roughly 1e-13 on subgroup pairwise contrasts, whose differencing amplifies rounding, up to roughly 1e-14 under *timetrends*, and larger still in relative terms where an estimate is itself near zero), because *margins* follows a different computational path when the variance is skipped. 

`nofastcollapse`: Turn the default cell collapse off, so that every *margins* call is evaluated on the full estimation sample. The option changes the summation order only, not the estimator. Use it when you need byte-for-byte reproduction of an archived result, when you are comparing output across runs at the last digit, or when memory is tight and the extra copy of the data held during the collapsed window is unwelcome. The price is the run time the collapse would have saved, quantified under **When it pays** below. It is forwarded to `permute()` and `bootstrap()` replications. The rest of this entry describes what you are turning off. 

**The cell collapse.** *wooldid* speeds up its *margins* calls by evaluating them on one row per cohort-by-period-by-treatment cell instead of on every observation. *wooldid*'s estimands are weighted averages of cell-level treatment effects, and within the conditions listed below the treatment derivative is constant inside such a cell, so the collapsed row - carrying the cell's summed weight - represents the whole cell exactly. The regression itself is untouched and still runs on the full data, so the coefficients, the variance matrix and the clustering are unaffected; only the averaging step that *margins* performs is handed a smaller dataset. The full data is restored before any plotting or display work. **Continuous treatments are covered too**: with *contreat()* the collapsed row additionally carries the cell's weighted mean of the treatment variable, which reproduces every reported effect exactly because each of them is linear in the dose inside a cell - the treatment-dummy derivative is *delta + beta*dose*, the dose derivative (*ame*) is constant, and *contreatelasticitytype(dyex)* is proportional to the dose. The forms that are **not** linear in the dose are listed below and turn the collapse off. **The collapse is on by default** whenever the request permits it, so there is nothing to type to obtain it, and `nofastcollapse` is the only way to change that. Where a request is ineligible the collapse is skipped and *wooldid* says so, in a caution naming the option responsible, as described under *What turns the collapse off*. The collapse composes with *fastnose*, is applied inside `permute()` and `bootstrap()` replications, and is recorded in the hidden macro *e(wdid_fcol)* (1 when it was applied, 0 when it was not), beside the scalar *e(wdid_fcncell)* - the collapsed cell count - and the macro *e(wdid_fcskip)*, which names the reason when an ineligible run did not collapse; *verbose* reports the decision as the run proceeds. 

**When it pays.** The saving grows with the number of observations per cell and with the size of the regression, and is unaffected by the choice of standard errors. On a 30,000-observation administrative panel with roughly 500 rows per cell it removed 35-60% of total run time; on a 475,000-row version, 56%. It pays for itself inside a single *margins* call at that scale - a bare *att* is already enough, with no event-study horizons requested: a vanilla *att* run with *contreat()* was 1.24 times faster on a 4,000-observation sample, 1.25 times on a 29,000-observation one and 1.33 times on a 145,000-observation one, so building the cell means does not eat the saving. Adding event-study horizons makes it a better deal rather than a worse one, and so does asking for more estimands: the same 29,000-observation run went to 2.0 times faster with all four. It gains little when there are only a handful of rows per cell (roughly four or fewer), where the collapse can cost slightly more than it saves, and it helps least on designs with very many cohorts and periods, where the absorbing regression rather than *margins* dominates the run. See Runtime and what drives it. 

**Results typically agree to machine precision but are not bit-identical.** Collapsing changes the order in which *margins* accumulates its averages, and the controls' contribution cancels algebraically between the treated and untreated predictions without cancelling bit for bit. If you diff output across runs, expect low-order digits to differ between a collapsed and an uncollapsed run; if you need byte-identical reproductions of a result produced without the collapse, run with `nofastcollapse`, which restores the summation order but not a computation that has itself changed since. 

On a panel whose predictions are of order 10, that is about 2e-15. Note this scales with the prediction, not with the estimate: an outcome with a very large mean raises *|xb|* and the absolute differences with it. 

Because the bound is absolute, what it means in **relative** terms depends on how big the estimate is. An ordinary coefficient - say an ATT of order 0.5 - agrees to roughly 15 significant digits (relative differences around 3e-15). An estimate sitting near zero, such as a small pre-treatment coefficient of order 0.01, can show a relative difference around 1e-13, purely because the denominator is small rather than because the error is larger. P-values in a far tail move more in relative terms - a p-value of 2e-07 moved by about 3e-11 relative - which is a property of tail probabilities, not of this option. 

**Continuous treatments carry a second rounding step, and a looser bound.** The figures above are for the binary case, where the collapsed row only re-orders the summation. With *contreat()* the row also carries the cell's weighted mean of the dose, which is itself a rounded quantity, so the bound is set by that mean rather than by one unit in the last place of the prediction. Two decimal orders of that headroom are spent on near-zero cells: an ordinary coefficient still agrees to about 15 significant digits. As in the binary case, no digit *wooldid* displays is affected. 

**Where *margins* is itself inexact, the agreement is looser.** With sampling weights, *controls()* and an event-study window, the collapsed and uncollapsed runs can differ by up to about 1e-6 relative on standard errors - enough to move the last printed digit of a standard error - and by more in relative terms on a p-value far in the tail; the point estimates still agree to machine precision. Neither run is exact there. *margins* builds the delta-method Jacobian by finite differences, and on these specifications that Jacobian carries rounding residue in columns whose exact derivative is zero. The residue depends on the control values of the rows *margins* is handed, so the collapse, which hands it one row per cell, draws a different residue rather than adding an error of its own: against an exactly computed Jacobian, either run can be the one further off. It is not specific to one estimation path: the largest differences measured, about 1e-6 relative on standard errors, were on the default *reghdfe* path with `cluster()`, *contreat()*, *controls()*, *timetrends* and sampling weights, and under *customvce()* the largest was about 2e-7. 

**When should you care?** For almost all work, not at all: outside the case just described no digit *wooldid* displays is affected, and the differences are several orders of magnitude below any sampling uncertainty you are reporting. Two situations warrant attention. First, if you compare output byte-for-byte across runs, or archive results for exact reproduction - then be deliberate about whether the collapse is on or off (`nofastcollapse`) in every run you compare, and bear in mind that a request outside the eligible set is run uncollapsed under a caution, and that a *subgroups()* run can switch to the full data part-way through (see below; *e(wdid_fcguard)* records it). Second, if your analysis differences two nearly equal estimates, or otherwise divides by a quantity that is near zero, since that amplifies the relative size of any rounding difference. 

A useful calibration for the second case: merely permuting the order of the rows in your dataset - leaving the data otherwise untouched, with the collapse off - moves the same near-zero coefficient about 19 times **more** than the collapse does. So if a conclusion is sensitive at this scale, it is already sensitive to the order your observations happen to be stored in, which is worth knowing whether or not the collapse is in use. 

**What turns the collapse off: the complete list.** There are three ways the collapse does not run, and this paragraph is the single place that enumerates them. **(1) You turn it off**, with `nofastcollapse`, and *wooldid* says nothing further about it. **(2) The request is ineligible**, in which case the collapse is **skipped** and the run proceeds on the full data exactly as it does under `nofastcollapse`. That skip is announced: *wooldid* prints a caution naming the option responsible and what it breaks, since a run that quietly declines to collapse is a run whose timing and last digits both change for a reason you cannot see. *safety(off)* does **not** silence the caution, and neither does anything but asking for `nofastcollapse` yourself. **(3) A *subgroups()* run sets it aside part-way through**, which is a screen rather than an eligibility rule and is described in the next paragraph. 

The ineligible list, grouped by the condition each one breaks. **The treatment effect is no longer constant inside a cell:** *interactivecontrols()*, which interacts the treatment dummy with a variable that varies within the cell; and, under *contreat()*, the treatment variable **also** appearing inside *controls()*, *coarsecohortcontrols()*, *fe()* or *ccc_absorb()*, which makes the dose derivative vary within the cell on a variable the collapse does not average.

Everything else is eligible, including several combinations that are easy to assume are not. *contreat()* is eligible on the default *dydx* scale and under *contreatelasticitytype(dyex)*, with every flavour of *contreatcontrols()* and with *ccnoabsorb*. *lattice()* and *makecfxplots* are eligible too: each splits a cell on a dose bin, so the bin joins the cell key - the lattice variable, and under *makecfxplots* the *cfxlattice()* variable - and each collapsed row stands for one bin of one cell. Poisson estimation is eligible, including with *contreat()* and with *contreatelasticitytype(dyex)*. So are *fastnose*, *jointtests*, *adversarial* and *altmethod_adversarial*, *esfixedbaseperiod* and *esrelativeto()*, *pretrendpower()*, *histogramcohorteffects*, *summarystats*, *gteffects* (which *wooldid* refuses alongside *contreat()* for a separate reason), *makeplots* and *saveplots()*, and the replications of `permute()` and `bootstrap()`, which are evaluated collapsed like any other run. 

****subgroups()** is eligible, but screened, for a subtler reason worth understanding.** When *margins* judges a subgroup-by-horizon effect not estimable it does not error - it computes the number anyway and flags it only by marking the name it returns, and that flag rests on a rank decision made at the last digit. wooldid's subgroup code is the one place that reads the flag, and it does so three separate ways: to drop the affected horizon with a caution, to skip the adversarial pre-trend test, and inside the subgroup joint tests. A fourth exact-zero branch is the adversarial line test's own rank truncation, at 1e-12 of the largest eigenvalue of the event-study covariance, which runs on every event study (subgroup or not) and is three orders tighter than the collapse screen's tolerance - so a residue-level direction is dropped identically collapsed or not. So it is the one place where the collapse's last-digit change in summation order can change **what** is reported rather than its low-order digits: a horizon that should be dropped can come back as a meaningless near-zero, or a reported joint test can turn to missing. The screen is a few matrix operations per call. If it finds nothing, the collapsed result stands. If it finds a singular direction, then under the default safety setting wooldid restores the full data at that point and evaluates that call and every remaining subgroup call on it, so everything downstream of the flag is exactly what `nofastcollapse` would have produced (the main results, and any subgroup horizons already read before the flag, were computed collapsed and agree with their full-data values to the last digit as described above); *verbose* reports when this happens. Under *safety(off)* wooldid instead keeps the collapsed rows, as requested, and prints a caution naming what it found, since in that situation which subgroup horizons and joint tests are reported can depend on the summation order. The outcome is recorded in the hidden macro *e(wdid_fcguard)*: empty when nothing was flagged, *fallback*, or *caution*. A fallback carries into the replications of both `permute()` and `bootstrap()`, which are otherwise evaluated collapsed. 

**When does the screen fire?** In practice when a contrast between two subgroups has **exactly** zero variance in the full data too, so the "significance" of that contrast rests on rounding residue whichever way it is computed. The common route is clustering on a variable that nests the cohort variable - *cluster(state)* where the cohorts are state-by-group cells, or clustering on the cohort itself - so that subgroups of identical cohort-and-period composition are perfectly correlated; a handful of coarse clusters, a subgroup that is an exact shifted copy of another, and a subgroup nested inside a unit reach the same place. wooldid's own identical-standard-error, all-zero-variance or not-estimable caution usually accompanies such a run but does not always (a robust variance on a saturated design, or clustering on the period, can be singular there without it), so the screen adds coverage. Healthy subgroup runs pass untouched: realistic heteroskedasticity leaves the screened variance ratios between 0.1 and 1, seven to eight orders of magnitude from the tolerance. The one way a healthy variance matrix reaches it is outcome scales differing between subgroups by more than about a factor of 1e4 (a variance ratio of 1e-9): the cost is then the speedup under the default safety, and under *safety(off)* a caution that overstates the situation. Non-subgroup runs are not exposed: no code outside the subgroup path reads a *margins* result name, and 123 non-subgroup comparisons on aggregating data showed no structural difference at all. (The adversarial rank truncation just described applies to non-subgroup event studies too, but it is a fixed rule of the test itself, not a read of a *margins* result name.) 

**Memory.** The full data is held aside while the collapsed window runs, so peak memory includes an extra copy of your dataset. This is immaterial for typical panels but worth knowing if you are already near your memory limit, particularly if you have issued your own `preserve` before calling *wooldid*; `nofastcollapse` removes the extra copy. 

### Runtime and what drives it

Users are often surprised by which specifications are slow, because the intuitive lever - the number of observations - is not the binding one.

**Cost is driven by the cohort-by-period cell count, not by sample size.** Write K for the number of interaction cells *wooldid* builds, which is roughly the number of distinct values of *i* times the number of relative periods in the event-study window. Runtime grows at about the 1.3rd to 1.7th power of K, while sample size, subgroup count and event-study width are each merely linear.

**What this implies in practice.** Coarsening *i* is normally the single largest speedup available: move to cohorts or treatment-onset groups and recover unit-level variation with *fe()*. Halving the number of observations, by contrast, buys little, because it leaves K untouched - this is also why subsampling is a poor way to shorten a long `bootstrap()` or `permute()` run, where shrinking the window or coarsening *i* works and dropping rows largely does not. Narrowing the event-study window helps in proportion to the horizons removed. 

**The expensive options.** *lattice()* is the steepest axis, at roughly the 1.9th power of K, and it shifts the dominant cost from *margins* into the absorbing regression; prefer the smallest *latticenum* that answers the question. The `bootstrap()` re-identification leg - required by *att_it*, *att_i* and *att_itime*, and taken by `boottstat` whenever `cluster()` is given - roughly doubles per-replication cost where the cluster nests *i*, because the re-identified copies present a larger cell count to the regression; where the cluster is finer than *i* or cross-cuts it the units are kept whole, the cell count does not grow, and the overhead is the physical frame alone. On the other side, *fastnose* skips the variance calculation that accounts for most of a *margins* call and is correspondingly worth several times over on runs where standard errors are not needed. The default cell collapse (see *nofastcollapse*) attacks the same cost from the other side - it shrinks the data each *margins* call sees rather than skipping the variance - and the two compose. The collapse is already on unless *nofastcollapse* or an ineligible option turns it off, so a run that must be evaluated on the full data (*unconditionalse*, *interactivecontrols()*, *contreatpoly()* above 1, or one of the other `contreat()` reads on the ineligible list under *nofastcollapse*) is the one paying the full per-row *margins* cost described here. `subgroups()` is **not** one of them: it collapses like any other run, and only sets the collapse aside part-way through if the estimability screen described under *nofastcollapse* fires. 

**Where the time goes.** Nearly all of a typical run is spent inside Stata's *margins*, and most of each *margins* call is the delta-method variance calculation rather than the point estimates. Very little time is spent in *wooldid*'s own code, which is why the levers that matter are the ones that change the size of the problem handed to *margins* - K above all - rather than anything about how the data are stored or passed in. 

### Technical Features

`update`: If specified, the command *wooldid,* *update* will update your local installation of *wooldid* based on the most recent edition of the program available on github. The new files are written to disk, but a running Stata session keeps using the copy of *wooldid* it has already loaded, so the update takes effect only after you restart Stata or run `discard`. 

`customw:eightmultiplier(varname)`: When computing various treatment effects using the margins calculation (see step 2 here), the weights normally used to produce the requested type of treatment effect (ATT, ATT_i, etc.) will be multiplied by this variable. When requesting an ATT without specifying sample weights, this is equivalent to estimating all regressions without weights, but then using these weights when averaging treatment effects to obtain the ATT. This allows production of certain bespoke types of treatment effects. Values must be non-negative on in-sample observations (negative values would silently corrupt the weight masses and estimates; they are rejected with an error, bypassable with *safety(off)*); missing values drop the observation. Under `impvalues` the reported event effects are equal-weight averages of per-cluster margins, so the *e(escomp)* weight-share columns describe sample weight composition, not the Ibragimov-Muller cross-cluster aggregation. 

`clean:matrices`: If specified, *wooldid* will clear out all matrices storied in STATA's main memory and in mata. Occasionally, *wooldid* enters a state where it repeatedly crashes for difficult to determine reasons (relating to *reghdfe* crashing and leaving certain content in mata). Doing this can sometimes recover *wooldid* from this problem. 

`regtol(#)`: Specifies the convergence tolerance to be used by *reghdfe* and *ppmlhdfe*. The argument is the negative base-ten exponent of that tolerance rather than the tolerance itself (*regtol(9)* means *tol(1e-9)*), so a larger number is a tighter tolerance and a slower, more precise fit; the *wooldid* default of 9 is one digit tighter than *reghdfe*'s own 1e-8. It reaches the *reghdfe*/*ppmlhdfe* path only. Under *unconditionalse* or *customvce()* the estimation runs through `regress` or `poisson` on explicit dummies, neither of which takes a convergence tolerance, so *regtol()* is accepted there and has no effect. The value is not range-checked, so a negative argument produces a malformed tolerance and an opaque failure inside the estimation command. By default, *reghdfe* sets a value of 8; *wooldid* has a default of 9. You may wish to set this higher or lower; higher may be advisable in settings where you ask *reghdfe* to absorb interaction terms with many continuous variables -- as tends to occur when using continuous treatment variables. 

`ccno:absorb`: Forces continuous treatment controls to be included in the main regression statement, rather than in the *absorb()* option, which is the default. Since the controls it relocates exist only under *contreat()* with *contreatcontrols()*, the option is inert and silent on a run with no continuous treatment whenever using the commands *reghdfe* or *ppmlhdfe* to estimate the underlying regression. Under certain conditions, this can improve estimation stability and avoid problems with variables being inappropriately dropped from the regression. 

`ccc_absorb()`: An alternate version of coarsecohortcontrols() that admits only continuous variables and passes the controls to the *absorb()* statement when using a high dimensional fixed effects estimator. This is often less efficient than the alternative approach in terms of computation time, but can be important in terms of memory efficiency in the main regression or under certain other circumstances. Note: be extra careful to ensure that specifications featuring ccc_absorb() are identified; not all cases of identification failure in this case are bound to be automatically detected. Under `contreat()`, naming the treatment variable itself inside *ccc_absorb()* puts the run on the ineligible list for the default cell collapse (see *nofastcollapse*), which turns the collapse off under a caution naming it. It is not refused with an event-study window, but it can collide with one: whenever the *ccc_absorb()* variables are constant within a cohort-by-period cell - a unit-level variable with a unit-level *i* is the usual case - the absorbed set takes in the cohort-by-period treatment interactions themselves, the fitted model then carries no coefficient at any relative period, and the run stops with an error naming *ccc_absorb()* as a likely cause. Where those variables vary within the cell the combination estimates normally. 

`ver:bose`: Prints the regression command *wooldid* uses and prints progress reports throughout the estimation process. The regression itself is run quietly on every estimation path, so its coefficient table is never displayed. This option also stores the underlying regression model used by *wooldid* to *___wooldidfullmodel*; this model can be accessed using *estimates restore*. 

`emptycellsoverride`: By default, *wooldid* tells Stata to drop interaction terms that correspond with empty cells, which is achieved by the command *set emptycells drop*, which improves estimation efficiency in this setting. This option overrides this behavior. This should only be of interest in conjunction with the option *verbose*.

## 12. safety()

`safety(off)` is the documented way to bypass a check (`contreatwithin` separately relaxes the estimability screens). What it touches, by what the check does: some refusals are lifted outright (unconditional standard errors with a cell-weighted estimand; a cohort identifier that is not a positive integer; negative weights; a lattice variable that is not a non-negative integer; onset not on the grid of `t`; the `maxvar` preflight), some are downgraded to cautions (`contreatwithin` with `byTime`; the subgroup containment screens, with or without `permute()`: the within-cell screen is skipped outright and the split-unit screen becomes a caution), the post-estimation collinearity screens are skipped entirely (so a design with an unidentified dose slope prints structural zeros for `ame` without a caution), a few advisory cautions are suppressed (the granular-`i` warning, unconditional standard errors with aweights), and four things are computed differently (continuous-treatment plots for the non-`att` estimands are drawn; degenerate subgroup contrasts are reported instead of blanked; split units enter each subgroup by weight share; `poisson`'s separated cells are excluded). The refusals that stand whatever `safety()` is set to include every other option-combination screen (among them `bootstrap(bayes)` with a cell-weighted estimand and the `impvalues` cluster-geometry screen), the missing-package check, `poisson`'s non-negativity requirement and every `fastnose` incompatibility. `contreatwithin` rewrites the working safety value to `off` for the estimability screens only; the data-integrity screens, the dose-slope screen and its interactive-control twin still read the value you typed.

Vocabulary: **refused** means the run stops with a named error; **reported without inference** means the estimate stands and its standard error, statistic, p-value and interval are blank in the tables and result matrices, never zero (`e(V)` carries a negative placeholder on those diagonals, and the raw `e(b)` can hold structural zeros); **cautioned** means everything is computed and printed with a line naming the risk and the remedy. If a run changes your specification (a raised `espostlength()`, dropped periods, dropped units), wooldid says so whatever `safety()` is set to.

The rest of this chapter is the help file's inventory.

### From the help file

`safety(on|off)`: When *safety(off)* is not explicitly specified, *wooldid* will caution the user or halt estimation when *wooldid* is likely to yield unreliable estimates. Only *on* and *off* are accepted values; anything else is rejected with an error. Most important among these checks are tests for whether or not a variable intended to capture the treatment effect in a given i-t cell was dropped from the underlying regression for multicolinearity, as may occur if one's fixed effects or continuous treatment control variables partial out one of the main effects. Identification failures of this sort will generally be detected, though not necessarily when the problem concerns continuous treatments being analyzed with a lattice or with polynomial terms. Default safety also screens for very granular *i* variables: when *i* takes more than 100 distinct values, *wooldid* cautions that runtime grows faster than linearly in the cohort-by-period cell count built from *i* and that the specification can also exceed Stata's variable limits (consider unit-level fixed effects via *fe()* plus a coarser *i*, such as treatment onset groups; see Runtime and what drives it), and if the count of variables the specification requires exceeds the current `maxvar` setting, *wooldid* halts with an informative error before estimation rather than failing mid-run with r(907). These checks are cheap. The saving can be worth having once a specification is settled and is being varied only slightly between runs. It is not a reason to disable them while a specification is still being developed, and the granular-*i* caution described in the Performance chapter is suppressed along with everything else. *wooldid* also cautions, regardless of the *safety()* setting, when *jointtests* is combined with analytic clustered SEs clustered at the level of the cohort variable *i* or above, where the joint test p-values are severely oversized (see *jointtests*); the tests are computed and printed either way, and the caution names the remedies (*permute()*, bootstrap-based clustered SEs, or clustering strictly finer than the cohort-by-period cells, qualified there by how many distinct clusters the finer variable leaves inside each cell). Under `bootstrap()` or `permute()` the screen does not run at all, since the joint tests are then bootstrap or permutation quantities rather than analytic ones. 

**The refuse / caution / skip policy.** *wooldid* has one vocabulary for what it does when a specification is questionable, and it is worth stating once because the choice between the three is not arbitrary. **Refused** means the run stops with a named error: the combination would produce a number that is wrong, or one whose meaning is undefined. **Reported without inference** means the estimate stands and the standard error, statistic, p-value and interval are blank: the point estimate is identified and the uncertainty around it is not, and blanking is how that is said - never a zero. **Cautioned** means everything is computed and printed, with a line naming the risk and the remedy. No accepted option is a silent no-op, and no check downgrades itself: *safety(off)* is the single documented way to move a refusal to a caution or to skip a check, and it moves nothing else. It has never suppressed a disclosure about what was estimated - if the run changed your specification, it says so whatever *safety()* is set to. 

**What *safety()* actually touches.** The option is not a single switch on a single check; it gates nearly sixty sites across the shell, the worker, the validator and a dozen subprograms. This is the published inventory, grouped by what each site DOES rather than listed one line at a time - the form that is usable, and the form that stays true as the code moves. It is also the prerequisite for ever narrowing the option. 

**First, three values are in play, and they are not the same.** What you typed; a **working** value, which *contreatwithin* together with *contreat()* silently rewrites to *off*, because that combination makes the base treatment effects irrelevant by design; and a preserved copy of your own value. Roughly half the sites gate on your own value and are therefore untouched by that rewrite - every data-integrity assert (onset constancy within units, weight sign, the lattice and cfxlattice domains, onset-period commensurability, the *customweightmultiplier* domain), the subgroup-containment rules, the dose-slope screen and its interactive-control twin, and both engines' replication settings, which inherit **your** value rather than the rewritten one. The rest gate on the working value and are relaxed by that rewrite whether or not you asked for it. 

**What default safety buys you, by what it does when it fires.** 

- ****refuses, and *safety(off)* proceeds**** *unconditionalse* with a cell-weighted estimand; the cohort identifier not being a positive integer; negative weights; a non-integer or negative *lattice()* or *cfxlattice()* value; onset not commensurable with the time variable; and the variable-count preflight that would otherwise fail mid-run at r(907). 

- ****refuses, and *safety(off)* downgrades to a caution**** *contreatwithin* with the *byTime* continuous-treatment control type; and, under `permute()` with `subgroups()`, the two design-population screens - a subgroup variable that varies within a cohort-by-period cell, and one that splits a unit across subgroups. 

- ****skips a check entirely**** The whole post-estimation collinearity family, including the screen that reports an unidentified dose slope as **missing** rather than as the regression's structural zero; the realized relative-time range asserts; and the control-availability count. With the dose-slope screen skipped, where the continuous treatment carries no identifying variation the *ame* rows print the regression's structural zeros instead of missing values, and without the caution that names them; that caution, on a run where it prints, says so in as many words - a column of zeros with no standard errors beside them is not a set of estimates. **Note** that the dropped-coefficient scan in this family (the dropped-dummy error, the interaction-drop caution and the *untreatedZero* dropped-slope refusal), the relative-time range asserts and the control-availability count gate on the **working** value, so *contreatwithin* with *contreat()* skips them too: *contreatwithin* declares that the base effects are irrelevant to the design, which is why it carries the relaxation. The dose-slope screen and its interactive-control twin gate on the value you typed, so under *contreatwithin* they still run. 

- ****suppresses a caution, changing nothing else**** The granular-*i* runtime warning; unconditional standard errors with aweights; periods with no in-sample control; the first-period and time-trend forcing notes; and the announcement that *espostlength* was raised. 

- ****changes what is computed**** Continuous-treatment effect plots for the non-ATT estimands are produced only under *safety(off)* - the default refuses to draw them and says so. The subgroup-contrast degeneracy site belongs here rather than among the caution suppressions: under default safety the flagged pairwise rows have their standard error, statistic, p-value and interval blanked in *e(pwsgs)* and the across-subgroup equality tests that cover them are posted as missing, while *safety(off)* reports all of them, with a caution of its own naming them as cancellation residue. A caution prints either way, in two different wordings, so what *safety(off)* changes here is the numbers rather than the volume. This site reads the value the user typed, not the working value *contreatwithin* rewrites. 

**What *safety(off)* does **not** buy.** About forty-five refusals stand whatever it is set to, and they are the ones that matter most: every option-combination and domain screen, the missing-package check, the *poisson* non-negativity requirement, and every *fastnose* incompatibility. That is the line the option draws. *safety(off)* means *"I accept a risk"*; it has never meant *"give me numbers I have been shown are wrong"*, and no future narrowing of the option should move a check across that line. 

**The engine-compatibility table.** One row per option the two engines treat differently, in one shape, with the reason attached. *refused* means an error at parse; *allowed* means it runs and does what its own entry says. 

- ****option**** **under** `bootstrap()` **/ under** `permute()`

- **`impvalues`, `unconditionalse`, `oldsyntax`** refused / refused. Each replaces or reshapes the inference the engine is there to produce. 

- **`customvce()`** refused / refused. The engine has to know what the replication's variance is; an arbitrary vce string is not inspectable. 

- **multi-way `cluster()`** refused / refused. Both engines resample or relabel **one** unit, so there must be exactly one. 

- **`suppresssgprimaryeffects`** refused / refused. Under `permute()` the suppressed subgroup entries are precisely what the replications permute. 

- **`fastnose`** refused / it **is** the default. Fast mode is what makes the permutation affordable; `permanalyticse` opts back in, and a user-typed `fastnose` is refused alongside it, as alongside `permtstat` and `permtci`. 

- **`contreatwithin`, `contreatcontrols(untreatedZero)`** allowed / refused. Replications reassign onset while the dose path stays fixed, which contradicts the dose-zero-outside-treatment premise. 

- **`pretrendpower()`** supported / refused. Roth's diagnostic consumes an event-study **covariance**. The bootstrap has one and is fed it; randomization inference produces a null distribution of a statistic, not a covariance of the estimates. A difference of kind. 

- **`simultaneousbands`** supported / refused. A confidence-band object; the permutation engine's multiplicity device is `permstepdown`. 

- **`permstepdown`** not available / opt-in.

- **the studentizing switch** `boottstat` / `permtstat`. Both studentize, for different reasons - see fact 2 above. 

- **`cluster()` nesting *i*** not required / required unless `permonsetcohorts`. The bootstrap re-identifies on the drawn cluster, so a cluster finer than *i* simply resamples inside the cohort. The permutation draws one onset per cluster and writes it to every row, so a unit spanning two clusters carries two onsets and is undefined. `permonsetcohorts` resolves that by rebuilding *i* from the drawn onsets. 

- **`jointtests`** fixed-metric rank test, or the bootstrap of the statistic under `boottstat` / fixed-metric rank test. Neither is a Wald test on *e(V)*. 

- **`adversarial`** bootstrapped only under `boottstat`; otherwise absent with a caution and *e(boot_adv_absent)* set, never analytic under a bootstrap header / a fixed-metric rank test. 

- **`histogramcohorteffects`** untouched under both: it draws the observed point estimates, which neither engine changes, and uses no covariance.

- **draw count** `reps()` required / `permreps()` required, and `permexact` enumerates instead. 

`oldsyntax`: In older versions, *wooldid* used the suffix *contemp* to flag the event study coefficient containing the period contemporaneous with treatment. This turned out to make *wooldid* relatively incompatible with the popular event study plot production program `event_plot`, so the *contemp* suffix has been replaced with the suffix *post0*. This option reverts *wooldid* to using the older coefficient syntax, for compatibility with any older code that relies on it. It cannot be combined with `permute()` or `bootstrap()`: each refuses *oldsyntax* with an error.

## 13. Stored results

### Where to look first

- `e(wdidmainresults)`: every pooled and event-study estimate with its inference, one row per equation-and-name pair (`main:att`, `pretreatment:att`, `ES_att:post0`, ...). `e(wdidsgresults_#)` and `e(pwsgs)` are the subgroup twins.
- `e(b)` and `e(V)`: the display vector and covariance, named `main_att`, `pretreatment_att`, `ES_att_pre#`, `ES_att_post#` (and `_att_it`, `_att_i`, `_att_itime`, `_ame` variants, `_s#` per subgroup). `e(V)` is valid for tests within one event-study family only; `e(wdid_vcvstate)` says what it is on a given run.
- `e(b_es_type)` and `e(V_es_type)`: the event-study vector with its genuine full covariance, the input for `lincom`, `test`, `honestdid` and Roth's `pretrends` (see the geometry conditions in the body of this chapter). `e(numpre_type)` is `honestdid`'s `numpre()`.
- `e(gteffects)`: the cohort-by-period building blocks (`i`, `onset`, `caltime`, `relyr`, `estimate`, `se`, `p`, interval, `inwindow`, `nobs`, and the `w_*` weight columns); the weighted mean of `estimate` under a weight column over the post-treatment rows reproduces that estimand exactly.
- `e(escomp)`: composition by horizon (cohorts, units, clusters, observations, weight shares), the first thing to read when an event-study profile jumps.
- Test results: `e(joint_horiz*)`, `e(adversarial_pval_*)` and `e(adversarial_line_*)`, `e(joint_*_allsgssame)`, `e(roth_*)`, `e(esband_*)`.
- Engine records: `e(boot_*)`, `e(bootdist)`; `e(perm_*)`, `e(permdist_b)`. Under `permute()` `e(p)`, `e(ci_l)` and `e(ci_u)` are posted for `esttab` and there is no `e(se)`.
- Replay: `wooldid` alone redisplays the stored tables from `e()` (not the plots or the estimation-time cautions), also after `estimates restore`; it refuses after a failed call until a call completes.

Recipes:

```stata
* honestdid (requires esfixedbaseperiod at the default esrelativeto(-1), no timetrends, consecutive horizons)
wooldid y i t ttre, att espre(4) espost(4) esfixedbaseperiod cluster(unit) fe(unit)
matrix b = e(b_es_att)
matrix V = e(V_es_att)
honestdid, numpre(`=e(numpre_att)') b(b) vcov(V) delta(rm) mvec(0.5(0.5)2) parallel(0)

* event_plot on the display vector: run it straight after wooldid, before any ereturn post
event_plot, stub_lag(ES_att_post#) stub_lead(ES_att_pre#) default_look

* a test across horizons of one family (after saving anything else you need from e())
matrix b = e(b_es_att)
matrix V = e(V_es_att)
ereturn post b V
test post0 = post1
```

The rest of this chapter is the help file's full inventory, family by family.

### From the help file

### Working With Output

*wooldid* generates output primarily in the form of matrices and scalars stored in e(), though all main results can also be accessed using standard utilities like *esttab*. Wherever a stored name is composed from a subgroup value or from a continuous-treatment plot name, a hidden twin or companion whose name would run past Stata's 32-character limit is skipped silently; the visible table is never affected. That covers the per-family bootstrap subgroup tables *e(boot_*_sgresults_*)* and the studentized *e(boot_tpctl_sgresults_*)*; the per-family bootstrap cfx twins *e(boot_*_**cfxname**)*, which are lost once the cfx name passes 22 characters, reachable under *makecfxplotsbysg* without any long subgroup value at all; the per-subgroup `permstepdown` tables; and the `simultaneousbands` companions, where the two lengths behave differently: a very long family key drops the band and its companions together, so the family simply never appears in *e(esband_families)*, while an intermediate one posts *e(esband_*type*)* without *e(esband_crit_*type*)* and *e(esband_n_*type*)*. The commands *est restore* and *est save* are compatible with *wooldid* for saving its output. The visible *wooldid* output in e() can be viewed using the command *ereturn list*; a number of the results described below are posted *hidden* - the preserved analytic covariances and degrees of freedom, the per-family bootstrap and permutation tables, the permutation kernel, the tail and comparator records, and *e(wdid_vcvstate)* and its companions - and those appear only under *ereturn list, all*, though each can be read by name (*di e(wdid_vcvstate)*) either way. 

The coefficient vector *e(b)* and its covariance *e(V)* carry these column names, which are what *esttab*, *coefplot*, `lincom`, `test` and the `permtstat`/`bootsave()` tables address a coefficient by: *main_att* (the headline average treatment effect on the treated), *pretreatment_att* (the pooled pre-treatment average), *ES_att_pre#* and *ES_att_post#* (the event-study horizons, numbered outward from the reference period), and for the other estimand types the same names with *att* replaced by *att_it*, *att_i*, *att_itime* or *ame*. Under `subgroups()` each subgroup's rows take the suffix *_s#*. 

The primary results are stored in the following matrices, scalars, and macros: 

- ***e(wdidmainresults)*** All primary and event study results. The columns are *estimate*, *se*, *t* (*z* under *poisson*), *p*, *lb_95ci*, *ub_95ci* and *relyr*, the two interval columns being named for the run's `level()`: `level(90)` gives *lb_90ci* and *ub_90ci*, and a fractional level writes its point as a *p*, so `level(97.5)` gives *lb_97p5ci*, the same rule *e(gteffects)* follows. Rows are equation-and-name pairs: the pooled estimands sit under the equations *main* and *pretreatment* (*main:att*, *pretreatment:att*, *main:ame*, and so on) with *relyr* missing, and the event-study rows sit under *ES_att*, *ES_att_it*, *ES_ame* and *ES_ame_it*, named *post#* and *pre#* and carrying their horizon in *relyr*.

- ***e(wdidsgresults_*sgcase*)*** Primary and event study results for the subgroup *sgcase*, if subgroups are used and presentation of their primary results has not been suppressed.

- ***e(pwsgs)*** Pairwise differences between the subgroup-specific treatment effects, if subgroups are used. The columns are *estimate*, *se*, *t* (*z* under *poisson*), *p*, *lb_95ci* and *ub_95ci*, named for the run's `level()` as elsewhere; there is no *relyr* column here, unlike *e(wdidmainresults)*. A row whose analytic contrast covariance is rank-deficient carries its point estimate with every inference column missing - see the Subgroups chapter. 

Returned per estimand type: *att* (binary) and, with `contreat()`, *ame*, plus *att_it* / *ame_it* for the *att_it*, *att_i*, and *att_itime* estimands (which share a single event-study horizon internally). With `subgroups()`, per-subgroup versions *e(b_es_*type*_s*sgcase*)* / *e(V_es_*type*_s*sgcase*)* are returned as well. After *ereturn post* these matrices are valid inputs for *lincom* and *test*. A **direct positional handoff** to *honestdid* (Rambachan-Roth robustness) additionally requires ALL of: `esfixedbaseperiod` with the default `esrelativeto(-1)`; no `timetrends`; at least one pre- and one post-treatment coefficient; horizons consecutive apart from the intended jump over the omitted -1 reference (checkable signature on the colnames: *pre1* absent, rightmost pre column is *pre2*, *post0* present, no other gaps); and a covariance with no structurally degenerate rows (see *e(es_ndegen_*type*)* below; honestdid inference inherits whatever VCE you specified). *numpre* is purely positional and cannot communicate gaps, non-(-1) references, or the (default) pooled cohort-specific reference — those geometries hand honestdid the wrong event-time geometry **silently** and need a user-supplied transformation. Check the regime via *e(estimate_type)* == "fixed_reference_period" and the numeric spacing via the *relyr* column of *e(escomp)*/*e(escomp_s*sgcase*)*. Where the dose-slope screen voids the ame block entirely (every treated cohort-period dose-slope term dropped for multicollinearity, as when the dose is constant within cohort), both *e(b_es_ame*)* and *e(V_es_ame*)* are withheld, because the captured ame points are the regression's structural zeros rather than estimates; the Caution that normally announces a coefficient vector posted without its covariance is deliberately suppressed on those families, so nothing in the output reports the absence either. A *honestdid* or *pretrends* handoff therefore finds no ame input at all on such a design, the att-side event-study objects being unaffected and the structural zeros surviving only in the raw *e(b)*. Under cell containment *e(b_es_ame*)* does still post. Otherwise the matrices are returned whenever at least one horizon has an identified variance; horizons whose standard errors are not identified then carry zero (structural-zero) or missing diagonal rows, flagged by a Caution and by *e(es_ndegen_*type*)* (the count of degenerate rows, posted only when such rows exist — its absence marks a clean covariance). When NO horizon has an identified variance (`fastnose`, typically `timetrends`, and any run whose estimation returns an all-zero covariance), the covariance is not returned but the coefficient vector still is, under a Caution naming what has no input on that run: the estimates remain meaningful even where their uncertainty is unavailable. Additionally, when the cell-containment caution fires (see the continuous-treatments standard-error caution above), *e(V_es_ame*)* is withheld — the captured covariance is not the sampling covariance of the ame estimates — while *e(b_es_ame*)* still posts; att-side event-study objects are unaffected. Separately, *contreatcontrols(byCohort)* requires each treated cohort's untreated/reference-period observations to carry variation in the continuous treatment (that variation is all that identifies the cohort-specific baseline dose slope): wooldid refuses with r(459), naming the offending cohort, when a treated cohort has none, and prints a computational-fragility caution (suggesting a larger *regtol()*) when that variation is below 1/1000 of the sample standard deviation of the treatment variable.

- ****Design conditions for every consumer of** *e(V_es_*)*** The event-study covariance is a delta-method object carried out of the `margins` call, so it inherits the design's **identification**, not only its arithmetic - and neither condition below is visible in the matrix itself. **One:** the treated cells must not be absorbed by their own terms. Where a treated (*i*, relative-period) cell lies wholly inside one cluster of `cluster()`, the cluster-robust covariance cannot see that cell's own variation; *wooldid* cautions on exactly that predicate (see `jointtests`), and the same predicate governs how much the matrix says about the treated horizons at all. **Two - singleton-cell designs:** with a unit-level *i*, each treated cohort-by-period cell is a single observation, the treated residuals vanish, and the covariance carries control-cluster noise only. *honestdid* and `pretrendpower()` consume this matrix directly and inherit both conditions; a coarser *i* (cohorts, or treatment-onset groups) with unit fixed effects supplied through `fe()` is the design that satisfies them. 

- ***e(numpre_*type*)*** The number of pre-treatment relative periods (relative year < 0) in the corresponding event-study vector, provided for use as *honestdid*'s *numpre()* argument (subject to the geometry conditions in the *e(b_es)* entry above).

- ***e(gteffects)*** The cohort-by-relative-period building-block table produced by the *gteffects* option; see that option's description for the column layout. Alongside it, *e(gt_ncontained)* counts how many of those cells lie wholly inside a single cluster (or, under `robust`, inside a single observation) and so contribute no independent residual unit to the variance that priced them: those cells' *se*, *p* and interval columns are understated by a design-dependent factor and do not cover at their nominal level, while the aggregate estimates built from the same cells are unaffected. The scalar is posted only when at least one such cell was found, so its absence on a run that could have looked means none were.

- ***Bootstrap inference*** When `bootstrap()` is used: scalars *e(boot_reps)*, *e(boot_completed)*, *e(boot_failed)*, *e(boot_level)*, *e(boot_N_V)* (replications behind the covariance), *e(boot_v_posted)*, *e(boot_V_shortfall)* (set to 1, with a caution, when that covariance rests on fewer than half the completed replications, so the disclosure survives into a replayed estimate), *e(boot_sesmall)* under `boottstat` (how many completed replications reported at least one standard error below 1e-5 of the outcome's own standard deviation, beside *e(boot_novar)* for the stricter cut that excludes a draw outright) and, on a clustered run carrying a cell-weighted estimand or `boottstat`, *e(boot_reid)* (1 where drawn copies of a cluster were given fresh unit codes, 0 where every unit kept its own) beside *e(boot_nstraddle)* (how many *i* values in the estimation sample straddle two or more `cluster()` values, which is the property that decides it) and, only when at least one covariate term named *i* and was rewritten to the fresh unit code, *e(boot_covsub)* (the count); macros *e(boot_method)*, *e(boot_stat)* (*z* or *t* under `bootdisplay(wald)`, *z_wald* or *t_wald* under the percentile and bias-corrected displays, *t_pt* under `boottstat`), *e(boot_save_rc)* (the return code of a failed `bootsave()` write, present only then), *e(boot_seed)* (under `rseed()`), *e(boot_rngstate)* (the random-number state before the first draw), *e(boot_alpha)* (under `bootstrap(bayes)` only) and *e(boot_ties)* (under `ties` only); matrices *e(V_boot)*, *e(b_boot)* (replication means), *e(bias_boot)*, *e(boot_navail)* (available replications per estimate), *e(boot_ci_normal)*, *e(boot_ci_percentile)*, *e(boot_ci_bc)*, *e(boot_z0)*, under `bootdisplay(bca)` also *e(boot_ci_bca)*, *e(boot_p_bca)*, *e(boot_accel)* and the scalar *e(boot_jk_completed)*, and - up to 10,000 completed replications - *e(bootdist)*, the replication matrix itself (above the cap a caution says it is not posted, and `bootsave()` writes the draws regardless). The analytic covariances the bootstrap replaced are kept as hidden *e(V_analytic)* and *e(V_es_analytic_*)*. *e(boot_ves_failed)* lists the event-study families (*att*, *att_it*, *ame*, *ame_it* and their *_s#* subgroup twins) whose *e(V_es_*)* could not be rebuilt from the replications and therefore remains the analytic matrix under a bootstrap header; the equivalent test is the absence of that family's hidden *e(V_es_analytic_*)*, which is created only on the success branch. The consumers of that matrix, `pretrendpower()` in particular and the *honestdid* handoff with it, are skipped for the listed families rather than run against an analytic covariance. The macro is what survives into a replayed or restored estimate once the run-time caution has scrolled away; its absence means every family was rebuilt. Three inference families are stored on every bootstrap run whichever one is displayed, and a fourth, BCa, whenever `bootdisplay(bca)` is requested, the acceleration being computed only on request; the BCa tables are *e(boot_bca_mainresults)*, with *e(boot_bca_sgresults_*sgcase*)* and *e(boot_bca_pwsgs)* alongside, the *_sgresults_** and *_pwsgs* counterparts existing for all four families. Of the three standing ones: *e(boot_p_percentile)* and *e(boot_p_bc)* hold the percentile and bias-corrected p-values, and the hidden *e(boot_pctl_mainresults)* (also posted under the derivable name *e(boot_percentile_mainresults)*, so that *e(boot_*``e(boot_display)'`*_mainresults)* resolves for every display including the default), *e(boot_bc_mainresults)* and *e(boot_wald_mainresults)* - with their *_sgresults_** and *_pwsgs* counterparts - hold the complete tables under each. The reported standard error is the bootstrap standard deviation under all three, so it does not vary with `bootdisplay()`; under `boottstat` it is the analytic standard error instead, the displayed family is the studentized one (the hidden *e(boot_tpctl_*)* tables), and the coefficient-draw Wald, percentile and bias-corrected tables remain stored for reference but are not displayable. A `boottstat` run also stores *e(boot_tstud)* (rows *n*, *t_obs*, *p_pctl*, *qlo_pctl*, *qhi_pctl*, *live*, one named column per coefficient), *e(bootdist_se)* (the replication standard errors, on the same row mask as *e(bootdist)*) - and note that these are the raw harvest: a replication whose variance estimate collapsed is withheld from the studentized kernels but its sampled standard error is still reported here and in any `bootsave()` file, so reproducing a printed studentized interval by hand from this matrix requires applying the same viability rule (*e(boot_novar)* counts the withheld draws), the hidden *e(boot_ase_obs)* (the observed analytic standard errors by name), *e(boot_tstud_pwsgs)* and *e(bootdist_pwse)* for the contrasts, *e(boot_jointW)* (the replication joint statistics, one named column per test) and *e(boot_advW)* (the replication adversarial statistics) beside *e(boot_advWobs)* (their observed twins), so every reported studentized number is reconstructable from *e()* by name. The *gteffects* and *e(cfx*)* cell tables are stored per family as the hidden *e(boot_pctl_gteffects)*, *e(boot_bc_gteffects)*, *e(boot_wald_gteffects)* and *e(boot_pctl_**cfxname**)* etc., with *e(boot_gteffects_family)* recording the displayed one; the adversarial p-value's absence without `boottstat` is recorded in *e(boot_adv_absent)*. The macro *e(boot_display)* records which family was displayed. The bootstrap analogue of the permutation tail record is the hidden *e(boot_ptails)* (rows *p_lo*, *p_hi*, *se_p*, *cp_lo*, *cp_hi*: the one-sided percentile shares about 0, the Monte-Carlo standard error of the percentile p-value and its `level()` Clopper-Pearson interval, one named column per coefficient), and the rep-0 comparator vector is the hidden *e(boot_comparator)* - the counterparts of *e(perm_p_lo)*/*e(perm_p_hi)*, *e(perm_p_se)*/*e(perm_p_ci)* and *e(perm_comparator_b)*. *e(boot_jt_family)* records how the joint and equality tests were formed (*studentized* under `boottstat`, *fixedmetric* otherwise), with the replication statistics in *e(boot_jointW)* / *e(boot_sgW)* and the observed ones in *e(boot_jointWobs)* / *e(boot_sgWobs)* (*e(boot_sgWobs)* without `boottstat`); when some joint or equality test could not be computed, *e(boot_jt_absent)* or *e(boot_sgjt_absent)* is set, so the disclosure survives replay. Those two flags say **that** a test was withheld; *e(boot_sgjt_reason)* says **why**, carrying one or more of *nostatistic* (attempted on the fixed metric and no statistic survived - fewer than two complete replications carry every subgroup level, or the bootstrap covariance of those levels is rank-deficient), *nostudentizedinputs* (under `boottstat`, the observed or per-replication pairwise standard errors the studentization needs were unavailable) and *unmapped* (the tested columns could not be matched to replication columns). The run says the same thing in a caution at the time; the macro is what survives into a replayed estimate. Absence of the flag means nothing was withheld, not that nothing was attempted. *e(boot_bc_absent)* is the same kind of record for the bias-corrected family, one level down: it lists the COEFFICIENTS whose *z0* was undefined because every completed draw fell on one side of the rep-0 comparator, and whose *e(boot_z0)*, *e(boot_ci_bc)*, *e(boot_p_bc)* and BCa twin are missing for that reason while their percentile and Wald entries stand; its absence means the family was withheld nowhere. The scalar *e(boot_df)* records the degrees of freedom in force whenever the reference distribution is a t - under `bootdf()` and equally under its default, which is `bootdf(clusters)` when `cluster()` was given and `bootdf(normal)`, which posts no *e(boot_df)* at all, otherwise. Under `boottstat` the scalar *e(boot_tstat)* is 1 and *e(boot_stat)* reads *t_pt*; the studentized kernel is *e(boot_tstud)* (described above), *e(bootdist_se)* holds the replications' own standard errors beside *e(bootdist)*, and the coefficient-draw tables stay stored but undisplayed. *e(df_r)* is aligned with the reference distribution actually reported, so a `test` or `lincom` run by hand after a bootstrap fit reproduces the `bootdisplay(wald)` p-values under every `bootdf()` setting - and note that *e(V)* and *e(df_r)* carry the standard-deviation family whichever family is displayed, so under the default percentile display they reproduce the stored *e(boot_wald_mainresults)* numbers rather than the columns printed in the tables - and each IDENTIFIED diagonal of *e(V)* is now that estimate's reported bootstrap standard error, squared, on a lossy run as well as a clean one. The two are built from different replication sets (each standard error from the replications in which its own column was available, the covariance from the rows complete across every reported estimate), and the covariance is rescaled so its diagonal agrees with the printed column. That rescaling is a congruence transform, so every listwise correlation is preserved exactly; a column for which the listwise pass found no variance at all is not identified, keeps wooldid's missing-variance sentinel, and is not rescaled: under `bootdf(clusters)` or an explicit integer it holds those degrees of freedom, and under `bootdf(normal)` it is set to missing so that `test` and `lincom` fall back to chi-squared and z, matching the reported statistics. The analytic residual degrees of freedom the bootstrap replaced are kept as hidden *e(df_r_analytic)*, alongside the analytic covariances. 

- ***Permutation inference*** When `permute()` is used, the reported tables themselves carry the permutation inference (columns *se_analytic*, *t_perm*, *p_perm* and the `level()` interval), and the hidden *e(perm_analytic_mainresults)*, *e(perm_analytic_sgresults_*)*, *e(perm_analytic_pwsgs)* and *e(perm_analytic_**cfxname**)* keep the tables as the observed run posted them (a hidden twin whose name would exceed Stata's 32-character limit - a subgroup value of 9 or more digits, or a long cfx name - is skipped; the visible table is unaffected. For the cell-draw twins each pair is guarded separately: a cfx name of 22-23 characters keeps *e(permdist_*)*/*e(permcmp_*)* and loses only the `permtstat` se twins). Stripe-aligned surfaces, one named column per coefficient of *e(b)*: *e(perm_p)* (the equal-tailed permutation p, or the absolute-value p under `permabsp`), *e(perm_ci)* (rows *ll*/*ul*, the shift interval), *e(perm_se_shift)* (the interval-implied standard error), *e(perm_p_lo)* and *e(perm_p_hi)* (the one-sided shares), *e(perm_stepdown)* and its family/pre/subgroup variants (the max-|studentized| step-down over a family's horizons, under `permstepdown`), *e(perm_p_se)* and *e(perm_p_ci)* (the Monte-Carlo standard error of the p-value's share and its `level()` Clopper-Pearson interval), *e(perm_sd)* (SD of the permutation distribution), *e(perm_n)* (replications carrying the element), *e(perm_nullband)* (rows *lo*/*hi*: the equal-tailed `level()` quantiles of the null draws), *e(perm_comparator_b)* (the rep-0 comparator vector), the hidden *e(perm_kernel)* (every row of the per-element kernel, by name) and *e(perm_ase_obs)* (the observed analytic standard errors), and - up to 10,000 completed replications - the draw matrices *e(permdist_b)* (and *e(permdist_se)* under `permtstat`). Test statistics: *e(perm_jointW)*, *e(perm_sgW)* and *e(perm_advW)* (the replication statistics behind the joint, equality and adversarial p-values, one named column per test), their observed twins *e(perm_jointWobs)*, *e(perm_sgWobs)* and *e(perm_advWobs)* (the observed statistic each p ranks; the p is reconstructable from the pair), the scalar *e(perm_adv)* (= 1 when the adversarial line tests were formed from the permutations) and the matrix *e(perm_pwsgs_kernel)* (the pairwise subgroup contrasts' kernel); *e(perm_comparator_se)* (the rep-0 analytic standard errors under `permtstat`); the hidden cell-draw twins *e(permdist_**cfxname**)*, *e(permcmp_**cfxname**)* and, under `permtstat`, *e(permdistse_**cfxname**)*/*e(permcmpse_**cfxname**)* behind the continuous-treatment plot cells; the macro *e(perm_offcentre)* (elements whose shift interval was blanked: columns of *e(b)*, then pairwise subgroup contrasts by their *e(pwsgs)* row name); the tests themselves post under the analytic names (*e(joint_horiz)* etc., *e(joint_*_allsgssame)*, *e(adversarial_pval_*)*). Run-level: *e(perm_reps)*, *e(perm_completed)*, *e(perm_failed)*, *e(perm_npossible)* (also as the string *e(perm_npossible_str)*), *e(perm_level)*, *e(perm_exact)*, *e(perm_blank)* (structural-zero ame cells excluded), *e(perm_precaveat)* (1 when the permuted coefficient set contains any pre-treatment row (a pretreatment-equation estimate, or any horizon at a negative relative year) and 0 otherwise; when it is 1 the staggered-design sharp-null caveat described under `permute()` in Standard Errors applies, and this scalar is what replays that printed caution into a restored estimate), *e(perm_gt_blank)*, *e(perm_cfx_blank)*, *e(perm_analyticse)*, *e(perm_absp)*, *e(perm_irebuilt)* (1 when the replications rebuilt *i* from the onsets they drew under `permonsetcohorts`, 0 when that option was given with `cluster()` already at *i*, where the rebuild is a no-op; not posted without the option); macros *e(perm_method)*, *e(perm_stat)* (*b* or *t*), *e(perm_pconv)* (*equaltailed*, the default; *absolute* under `permabsp`; *raw* under `permrawp`; *left* or *right* under `permside()`, the same five conventions mapped at Which p-value is which, of which at most one of the three options may be given), *e(perm_seed)* (under `rseed()`), *e(perm_rngstate)*, *e(perm_save_rc)* (the return code of a failed `permsave()` write, present only then) (the random-number state before the first draw) and *e(perm_incomplete)*, *e(perm_addone)* (the elements whose p-values took the add-one form). The legacy pooled-estimand scalars *e(perm_p_*est*)* (now the same p the table carries, in the convention in force), *e(perm_bandlo_*est*)*/*e(perm_bandhi_*est*)* (the `level()` null band - the region the statistic occupies under the null, NOT an interval for the estimand; the older names *e(perm_lb_*est*)*/*e(perm_ub_*est*)* hold the same two numbers and are deprecated, since lb/ub elsewhere in wooldid means a genuine interval bound), *e(perm_sd_*est*)* and, under `permtci`, *e(perm_tlb_*est*)*/*e(perm_tub_*est*)*, the estimand-keyed comparator *e(perm_comparator)* and the estimand-keyed draw matrix *e(permdist)* (on the *e(perm_stat)* scale) are kept. Note that *e(p)*, *e(ci_l)* and *e(ci_u)* are also posted on every `permute()` run, under the names *estout* looks for ahead of anything derived from a covariance; see the `permute()` entry for what they hold and why no *e(se)* accompanies them. *e(permdist)* is **not** the counterpart of *e(bootdist)* despite the name: it carries one column per requested estimand on the statistic scale, where *e(bootdist)* is the full coefficient-draw matrix aligned to *e(b)*. The counterpart is *e(permdist_b)*, and *e(permdist)* is deprecated in favour of it.

- ***e(escomp)*** Event-study composition diagnostics, one row per reported horizon (rows align one-for-one with the ES rows of *e(wdidmainresults)*; horizons without a surviving coefficient get no row). Columns: *ncohorts* (distinct treatment-onset values contributing), *nunits* (distinct units of *i* — equivalently, the number of unit-by-period coefficients averaged at that horizon; that identity is enforced for plain binary treated-cell coefficients under default safety by the hard dropped-coefficient error — it can fail under *safety(off)* or *contreatwithin*, and in continuous/interacted designs the screen cautions rather than errors), *nclusters* (distinct clusters, when a single `cluster()` variable is used; missing otherwise), *nobs* (estimation-sample observations), *sumw_att* (total *att*-style weight: sample weight times any `customweightmultiplier()`), *wshare_att* and *wshare_att_it* (the horizon's share of the reported-window treated weight under the *att* and *att_it* weighting concepts respectively; each column sums to 1 across the reported horizons), and *relyr*. With `subgroups()`, per-subgroup versions *e(escomp_s*sgcase*)* are returned as well. Composition shifts across horizons are a common cause of jumpy event-study profiles; note this is a descriptive counts-and-totals screen, not a test — flat composition columns rule out changes in these **reported** summaries (a cohort entering or leaving, a unit-count or weight-mass shift), but they do not rule out turnover of **which** units contribute at unchanged counts (identities are not stored), moving columns do not by themselves establish composition bias (that additionally requires effect heterogeneity across the cohorts entering or leaving), and composition change **within** a coarse *i* is visible only through *nobs*/*sumw_att*, not the count columns. The table is displayed when `summarystats` is specified.

- ***e(escomp_refN)*, *e(escomp_refnunits)*, *e(escomp_refncoh)*, *e(escomp_refW)*** Observation count, distinct treated units, distinct onset cohorts, and total *att*-style weight in the complete reference-normalization pool. Under the pooled regime that pool is the union of treated rows earlier than the requested pre-window (*relyr* < -*esprelength*) **and** each treated unit's forced first in-sample observation (first two under `timetrends`) — the forced rows exist even when the requested window covers every period. Under `esfixedbaseperiod` it is the `esrelativeto()` period plus, under `timetrends`, the immediately preceding trend-anchor period (two relative periods, not the named reference alone). Available under both regimes.

- ***e(escomp_trtN)*, *e(escomp_trtW)*** Observation count and total *att*-style weight over the entire treated estimation sample, for assessing how much treated weight lies outside the reported event-study window.

- ***e(anticipation)*** The anticipation shift applied to the treatment onset, returned when `anticipation()` is active.

- ***e(roth_power_*type*)*** With `pretrendpower()`: one row per requested power level, columns *power*, *mdt_wald*, *mdt_nis* (the minimum detectable linear pre-trend under the joint-Wald and any-individually-significant pre-tests respectively), and *bias_wald*, *bias_nis* (the bias such a trend would imply for that block's pooled estimand if it continued post-treatment, i.e. mdt × (hbar − reference); for *att_i*/*att_itime* requests the att_it block stands in and its bias columns describe att_it weighting only). Companion scalars: *e(roth_slope_*type*)*, *e(roth_slope_se_*type*)*, *e(roth_slope_ll/ul_*type*)* (the GLS pre-trend slope and its `level()` confidence interval (the run's own level, `c(level)` by default and so normally 95), built from a *t* critical value where residual degrees of freedom exist and a normal one otherwise), *e(roth_prep_*type*)* (the joint pre-trend test p-value), *e(roth_hbar_*type*)* (the estimand-weighted mean post-treatment relative period over the full post support of the estimation sample), *e(roth_npre_*type*)*, *e(roth_keff_*type*)* (pre-period count and effective rank), *e(roth_W_*type*)* (the joint pre-trend test statistic), and *e(roth_nissize_*type*)* (the actual size of the any-individually-significant pre-test under the normal reference). Where the pre-period covariance came back rank-deficient, the hidden *e(roth_cellmax_*type*)* records the largest number of observations any treated (*i*, relative-period) cell holds, which separates the two causes the caution distinguishes: a value of 1 is a singleton-cell design, in which the treated residuals are identically zero and the deficiency is structural, while a larger value says the rank loss came from somewhere else. *e(roth_ok)* = 1 marks a completed diagnostics pass: a type absent from the roth_* results was then legitimately skipped or unavailable, and roth_ok = 1 with no *e(roth_power_*)* matrix at all means the diagnostics were requested but none were computable (that caution is replayed). A roth_* object present **without** *e(roth_ok)* marks an interrupted pass; replay then prints a caution and does not render the partial block.

In addition to these core results, the following materials are also returned in e(): 

- **Joint Tests by Horizon** Joint tests for the statistical significance of pre-treatment relative time period-specific event study effects are presented in the scalars e(joint_horiz) (ATT), e(joint_horiz_i) (the shared ATT_it / ATT_i / ATT_itime horizon block), e(joint_Choriz) (AME; continuous treatments only), and e(joint_Choriz_i) (AME_it; continuous treatments only). What the p-value is depends on the inference engine: on an analytic run it is the p of *margins*' `contrast(overjoint)` test over the pre-treatment horizons; under `bootstrap()` it is an add-one rank p of the replication statistics, of the family named by *e(boot_jt_family)* (*studentized* or *fixedmetric*), the statistics themselves in *e(boot_jointW)*/*e(boot_jointWobs)*; under `permute()` it is a rank p of the permutation statistics in *e(perm_jointW)*/*e(perm_jointWobs)*, in the add-one form unless the observed assignment is itself one of the draws or the enumeration is exact. The three are not comparable to one another. 

- **Joint Tests by Subgroup & Time Period** Joint tests for the statistical significance of pre-treatment relative time period-specific event study effects are presented by subgroup, when used, in e(joint_horiz_s*sgcase*), e(joint_horiz_i_s*sgcase*), e(joint_Choriz_s*sgcase*), and e(joint_Choriz_i_s*sgcase*), where *sgcase* is the value of the subgroup. These are e() *scalars* on an analytic run and e() *local macros* under `bootstrap()` and `permute()`; an *e(**name**)* reference reads either, but code that keys on the type - `confirm scalar`, or filtering *ereturn list* - must expect both. The engine-dependent p-value conventions described just above apply here too. 

- **Adversarial Line Tests:** P-values from the composite-null tests that the relative-time period treatment effect estimates and the reference period fall on the same line are found in the scalars *e(adversarial_pval_att)*, *e(adversarial_pval_ame)*, *e(adversarial_pval_att_s[SG])*, etc.; they are posted only under *esfixedbaseperiod* and only when the event-study covariance is not degenerate (a caution is printed otherwise). The display-oriented fitted line is always returned in matrices named *e(adversarial_line_att)* and *e(adversarial_line_att_it)*, joined under `contreat()` by *e(adversarial_line_ame)* and *e(adversarial_line_ame_it)*, with per-subgroup twins named *e(adversarial_line_*type*_s*sgcase*)* exactly as the p-value scalars are, and with columns *slope* and *intercept_rt0*; the line matrix and the p-value scalar are gated independently, so the line can be present where the p-value is not; *intercept_rt0* is the fitted value at numeric relative time 0, regardless of wooldid's internal nonnegative time coding.
